2357:     private int calculateEvidenceMomentum(List<TradeSignal> observedDescending) {
2358:         if (observedDescending == null || observedDescending.size() < 2) return 0;
2359: 
2360:         List<TradeSignal> sequence = new ArrayList<>(observedDescending);
2361:         Collections.reverse(sequence); // oldest -> newest
2362:         if (sequence.size() > EVIDENCE_MOMENTUM_WINDOW) {
2363:             sequence = new ArrayList<>(sequence.subList(sequence.size() - EVIDENCE_MOMENTUM_WINDOW, sequence.size()));
2364:         }
2365: 
2366:         int momentum = 0;
2367:         for (int i = 1; i < sequence.size(); i++) {
2368:             TradeSignal previous = sequence.get(i - 1);
2369:             TradeSignal current = sequence.get(i);
2370:             int previousStrength = decisionStrength(strongerDecision(previous.getDecision(), previous.getOriginalDecision()));
2371:             int currentStrength = decisionStrength(strongerDecision(current.getDecision(), current.getOriginalDecision()));
2372:             int delta = currentStrength - previousStrength;
2373:             int recencyWeight = Math.min(3, i);
2374:             momentum += delta * 3 * recencyWeight;
2375:         }
2376: 
2377:         TradeSignal latest = sequence.get(sequence.size() - 1);
2378:         int latestStrength = decisionStrength(strongerDecision(latest.getDecision(), latest.getOriginalDecision()));
2379:         momentum += latestStrength * 2;
2380:         return Math.max(-EVIDENCE_MOMENTUM_CAP, Math.min(EVIDENCE_MOMENTUM_CAP, momentum));
2381:     }
2382: 
2383:     private int decisionStrength(SignalDecision decision) {
2384:         if (decision == null) return 0;
2385:         return switch (decision) {
2386:             case STRONG_BUY -> 2;
2387:             case BUY, WATCH -> 1;
2388:             case NEUTRAL -> 0;
2389:             case SELL -> -1;
2390:             case STRONG_SELL -> -2;
2391:         };
2392:     }

2394:     private int timeframeHealthContribution(TradeSignal signal, String interval) {
2395:         if (signal == null) return 0;
2396:         SignalDecision decision = strongerDecision(signal.getDecision(), signal.getOriginalDecision());
2397:         int base;
2398:         if (CONFIRMATION_INTERVAL.equals(interval)) {
2399:             base = switch (decision) {
2400:                 case BUY, STRONG_BUY -> HEALTH_5M_BUY;
2401:                 case WATCH -> HEALTH_5M_WATCH;
2402:                 case SELL -> HEALTH_5M_SELL;
2403:                 case STRONG_SELL -> HEALTH_5M_STRONG_SELL;
2404:                 default -> 0;
2405:             };
2406:         } else {
2407:             base = switch (decision) {
2408:                 case BUY, STRONG_BUY -> HEALTH_1H_BUY;
2409:                 case WATCH -> HEALTH_1H_WATCH;
2410:                 case SELL -> HEALTH_1H_SELL;
2411:                 case STRONG_SELL -> HEALTH_1H_STRONG_SELL;
2412:                 default -> 0;
2413:             };
2414:         }
2415:         return scaledHealthContribution(base, signal);
2416:     }
2417: 
2418:     private int scaledHealthContribution(int base, TradeSignal signal) {
2419:         if (base == 0 || signal == null) return 0;
2420:         int quality = Math.round((signal.getTotalScore() + signal.getConfidenceScore()) / 2.0f);
2421:         double factor = quality >= 80 ? 1.25 : quality >= 70 ? 1.0 : quality >= 60 ? 0.8 : 0.6;
2422:         return (int) Math.round(base * factor);
2423:     }

897:         // time. Therefore Production and Replay execute this exact same branch deterministically.

912:                         && opportunityAge.compareTo(ANCHOR_MAX_AGE) <= 0;
913:             }

919:                     reference = anchor;

921:                 }

923:                     reference = best;

925:                 }

933:         }

936:                 .divide(reference, 8, RoundingMode.HALF_UP)
937:                 .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100)).doubleValue();

954: 
955:         // FIX-091 / Fix 7: a prior high is considered a recent rejection only when price
956:         // subsequently backed away and the current entry is returning close to that same zone.
957:         // v1 is deliberately conservative: this only reduces Entry Quality; it never hard-vetoes.
958:         int rejectionCount = 0;
959:         double distanceFromRejectedHighPercent = 0d;
960:         BigDecimal recentRejectedHigh = null;

965:                         || s.getGeneratedAt().isAfter(current.getGeneratedAt()) || s.getLatestPrice() == null) continue;
966:                 if (s.getLatestPrice().compareTo(recentHigh) < 0) rejectionCount++;
967:             }
968:             distanceFromRejectedHighPercent = recentHigh.subtract(executionPrice).abs()
969:                     .divide(recentHigh, 8, RoundingMode.HALF_UP)
970:                     .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100)).doubleValue();
971:             if (rejectionCount >= 2 && distanceFromRejectedHighPercent <= 0.50d) {
972:                 recentRejectedHigh = recentHigh;
973:             }
974:         }
975: 
976:         int score = 100;
977: 
978:         if (expansionPercent > 8d) score -= 50;
979:         else if (expansionPercent > 5d) score -= 35;
980:         else if (expansionPercent > 3d) score -= 22;
981:         else if (expansionPercent > 1.5d) score -= 10;
982: 
983:         if (atrExtension > 6d) score -= 30;
984:         else if (atrExtension > 4d) score -= 20;
985:         else if (atrExtension > 2.5d) score -= 10;
986: 
987:         if (ageMinutes > 60) score -= 15;
988:         else if (ageMinutes > 40) score -= 10;
989:         else if (ageMinutes > 20) score -= 5;
990: 
991:         if (rr <= 0d || rr < 1d) score -= 30;
992:         else if (rr < 1.25d) score -= 15;
993:         else if (rr >= 2d) score += 5;
994: 
995:         if (recentRejectedHigh != null) {
996:             // FIX-091 / Fix 7 v1 is penalty/size-reduction only. A rejection penalty must not
997:             // by itself push an otherwise non-chase entry below the hard CHASE_ENTRY cutoff.
998:             int beforeRejectionPenalty = score;
999:             score -= RECENT_REJECTION_ENTRY_QUALITY_PENALTY;
1000:             if (beforeRejectionPenalty >= CHASE_ENTRY_CUTOFF) {
1001:                 score = Math.max(CHASE_ENTRY_CUTOFF, score);
1002:             }
1003:         }
1004: 
1005:         if ("HIGH_VOLATILITY".equals(String.valueOf(current.getMarketRegime()))) score -= 10;
1006: 
1007:         // FIX-055: STOP_EXPOSED remains a soft warning (TARGET_BLOCKED is still the
1008:         // hard liquidity veto), but an exposed stop must reduce entry quality and
1009:         // therefore position size or block a chase through the existing quality guard.
1010:         if (current.getLiquidityStatus() == LiquidityContextStatus.STOP_EXPOSED) {
1011:             score -= STOP_EXPOSED_ENTRY_QUALITY_PENALTY;
1012:         }
1013: 
1014:         if ("BREAKOUT".equals(String.valueOf(current.getMarketRegime()))
1015:                 && expansionPercent <= 3d && atrExtension <= 2.5d) score += 5;
1016: 
1017:         score = Math.max(0, Math.min(100, score));
1018:         String classification = score >= 85 ? "EXCELLENT_ENTRY"
1019:                 : score >= 70 ? "GOOD_ENTRY"
1020:                 : score >= 55 ? "ACCEPTABLE_ENTRY"
1021:                 : score >= CHASE_ENTRY_CUTOFF ? "LATE_ENTRY"
1022:                 : "CHASE_ENTRY";
1023: 
1024:         boolean moderateBtcConflict = current.getBtcContextStatus() == com.crypto.domain.BtcContextStatus.CONFLICT;
1025:         boolean transitionProbe = "TRANSITION_PROBE".equals(current.getEntryAuthority());
1026:         Integer authorityCap = current.getEntryAuthorityMaxPositionPercent();
1027:         return new EntryQuality(score, classification, expansionPercent, atrExtension, rr, ageMinutes,
1028:                 moderateBtcConflict, recentRejectedHigh, rejectionCount, distanceFromRejectedHighPercent,
1029:                 transitionProbe, authorityCap);

1145:     /**
1146:      * FIX-056: final execution-time revalidation. The signal decision remains immutable,
1147:      * but a wallet BUY must be re-checked against the current canonical market price before
1148:      * sizing/fill. This prevents a valid old signal from becoming a late chase by execution time.
1149:      */
1150:     public ExecutionDecision revalidateAtExecutionPrice(TradeSignal signal, ExecutionDecision approvedDecision, BigDecimal executionPrice) {
1151:         // FIX-122: recheck only stamped initial approvals; ADD approvals carry no stamp.
1152:         if (stopLossEvidence != null) approvedDecision = stopLossEvidence.recheck(signal, approvedDecision);
1153:         if (approvedDecision != null && !approvedDecision.allowed()) return approvedDecision;
1154:         if (signal == null || approvedDecision == null || !approvedDecision.allowed()
1155:                 || executionPrice == null || executionPrice.signum() <= 0) {
1156:             return ExecutionDecision.reject("EXECUTION_PRICE_INVALID",
1157:                     "A fresh positive execution price is required before wallet execution.");
1158:         }
1159:         if (signal.getStopLoss() != null && executionPrice.compareTo(signal.getStopLoss()) <= 0) {
1160:             return ExecutionDecision.reject("EXECUTION_PRICE_AT_OR_BELOW_STOP",
1161:                     "Current execution price is already at/below the signal stop; BUY cancelled.", approvedDecision.evidence());
1162:         }
1163:         if (signal.getTakeProfit() != null && executionPrice.compareTo(signal.getTakeProfit()) >= 0) {
1164:             return ExecutionDecision.reject("EXECUTION_PRICE_AT_OR_ABOVE_TARGET",
1165:                     "Current execution price has already reached/exceeded the signal target; BUY cancelled.", approvedDecision.evidence());
1166:         }
1167:         EntryQuality quality = assessEntryQualityAtPrice(signal, executionPrice);
1168:         return applyInitialEntryQualityGuard(approvedDecision, quality).withStopBoundary(approvedDecision.stopBoundary());
1169:     }

1814:         java.math.BigDecimal reward = signal.getTakeProfit().subtract(marketPrice);
1815:         if (risk.signum() <= 0 || reward.signum() <= 0) return 0d;
1816:         return reward.divide(risk, 8, java.math.RoundingMode.HALF_UP).doubleValue();
1817:     }
1818: 
1819:     /**
1820:      * Small exploratory entry for a proven pressure-reversal sequence while normal MTF
1821:      * confirmation is still lagging. This is intentionally NOT a BUY-promotion method.
1822:      *
1823:      * <p>Normal direct BUY validation already ran before this method. Therefore a BUY
1824:      * that belongs to the existing normal path can never use PRESSURE_PROBE_ENTRY as a
1825:      * fallback around normal validation. The probe is restricted to WATCH/NEUTRAL.</p>
1826:      *
1827:      * <p>A bearish 1h label is allowed only because the exposure is capped at 15% and
1828:      * PressureReadinessService must prove a completed burst/rejection/higher-low/rebuild
1829:      * sequence using only candles closed as-of the current signal timestamp. This does
1830:      * not weaken the normal 1h veto for normal-sized trades.</p>
1831:      */
1832: 
1833:     /**
1834:      * FIX-026 recovery-transition probe. Normal direct BUY and the proven pressure-probe
1835:      * route already had priority before this method. This is intentionally a small,
1836:      * state-transition exception for WATCH/NEUTRAL only; it never converts a bearish
1837:      * current signal and it never bypasses a hard risk/context veto.
1838:      */
1839:     private ExecutionDecision recoveryTransitionDecision(TradeSignal current, Evidence evidence) {
1840:         if (current == null || current.getGeneratedAt() == null) return null;

2139:     private boolean isDirectBuyCandidate(TradeSignal signal) {
2140:         return signal.isFinalEntryAllowed()
2141:                 && signal.isAtrImmediateEntryAllowed()
2142:                 && signal.getTotalScore() >= properties.minimumBuyScore()
2143:                 && isBullish(signal.getDecision());
2144:     }
2145: 
2146:     /**
2147:      * FIX-064 / ENA #703, PEPE #756, SUI #802:
2148:      * Derived BUY/WATCH fields can lag an exhausted or rejected price impulse. Before the three
2149:      * affected entry routes (SCOUT, DEFERRED_CONTINUATION and ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE) open fresh
2150:      * risk, inspect only candles that were fully CLOSED at signal.generatedAt. This deliberately
2151:      * does not alter market-regime categories, scores, BUY thresholds or accumulated-evidence
2152:      * semantics. Missing candle/ATR data fails open so data gaps cannot silently disable proven
2153:      * winning routes. Production and Replay use the same timestamp-bounded repository query.
2154:      */
2155:     private ImmediatePriceAction immediatePriceAction(TradeSignal signal) {
2156:         if (signal == null || signal.getGeneratedAt() == null || signal.getAtrAtSignal() == null
2157:                 || signal.getAtrAtSignal().signum() <= 0) {
2158:             return ImmediatePriceAction.supportive("raw candle/ATR unavailable; existing behavior preserved");
2159:         }
2160: 
2161:         List<Candle> candles = candleRepository.findClosedCandlesClosedAtOrBefore(
2162:                 signal.getSymbol(), EXECUTION_INTERVAL, signal.getGeneratedAt(),
2163:                 PageRequest.of(0, IMMEDIATE_PRICE_ACTION_LOOKBACK_CANDLES));
2164:         if (candles == null || candles.size() < 2) {
2165:             return ImmediatePriceAction.supportive("insufficient closed-candle history; existing behavior preserved");
2166:         }
2167: 
2168:         Candle latest = candles.get(0);
2169:         Candle previous = candles.get(1);
2170:         if (latest.getOpenPrice() == null || latest.getHighPrice() == null || latest.getLowPrice() == null
2171:                 || latest.getClosePrice() == null || previous.getClosePrice() == null) {
2172:             return ImmediatePriceAction.supportive("incomplete closed-candle OHLC; existing behavior preserved");
2173:         }
2174: 
2175:         BigDecimal atr = signal.getAtrAtSignal();
2176:         BigDecimal range = latest.getHighPrice().subtract(latest.getLowPrice()).abs();
2177:         double closeLocation = range.signum() == 0 ? 0.5d
2178:                 : latest.getClosePrice().subtract(latest.getLowPrice())
2179:                         .divide(range, 12, RoundingMode.HALF_UP).doubleValue();
2180:         closeLocation = Math.max(0d, Math.min(1d, closeLocation));
2181: 
2182:         double bearishBodyAtr = latest.getOpenPrice().subtract(latest.getClosePrice())
2183:                 .max(BigDecimal.ZERO).divide(atr, 12, RoundingMode.HALF_UP).doubleValue();
2184:         BigDecimal recentHigh = candles.stream()
2185:                 .map(Candle::getHighPrice)
2186:                 .filter(Objects::nonNull)
2187:                 .max(BigDecimal::compareTo)
2188:                 .orElse(latest.getHighPrice());
2189:         double recentHighFadeAtr = recentHigh.subtract(latest.getClosePrice())
2190:                 .max(BigDecimal.ZERO).divide(atr, 12, RoundingMode.HALF_UP).doubleValue();
2191: 
2192:         boolean closeBelowPrevious = latest.getClosePrice().compareTo(previous.getClosePrice()) < 0;
2193:         boolean bearishRejection = latest.getClosePrice().compareTo(latest.getOpenPrice()) < 0
2194:                 && closeBelowPrevious
2195:                 && bearishBodyAtr >= IMMEDIATE_PRICE_ACTION_MIN_BEARISH_BODY_ATR
2196:                 && closeLocation <= IMMEDIATE_PRICE_ACTION_MAX_CLOSE_LOCATION_FOR_BEARISH_REJECTION;
2197:         boolean exhaustedPop = closeBelowPrevious
2198:                 && recentHighFadeAtr >= IMMEDIATE_PRICE_ACTION_MIN_RECENT_HIGH_FADE_ATR
2199:                 && closeLocation <= IMMEDIATE_PRICE_ACTION_MAX_CLOSE_LOCATION_FOR_EXHAUSTED_POP;
2200: 
2201:         String summary = "closeLocation=" + format(closeLocation * 100d) + "%"
2202:                 + ", bearishBody=" + format(bearishBodyAtr) + " ATR"
2203:                 + ", fadeFromRecentHigh=" + format(recentHighFadeAtr) + " ATR"
2204:                 + ", closeVsPrevious=" + (closeBelowPrevious ? "LOWER" : "NOT_LOWER");
2205: 
2206:         if (bearishRejection || exhaustedPop) {
2207:             return ImmediatePriceAction.rejected(summary
2208:                     + (bearishRejection ? ", bearish rejection" : "")
2209:                     + (exhaustedPop ? ", exhausted-pop fade" : ""));
2210:         }
2211:         return ImmediatePriceAction.supportive(summary);
2212:     }
2213: 
2214:     private boolean isSupportiveCurrentSignal(TradeSignal signal) {
2215:         if (isBullish(signal.getDecision()) || isBullish(signal.getOriginalDecision())) return true;
2216:         return signal.getDecision() == SignalDecision.WATCH
2217:                 && signal.getTotalScore() >= WATCH_EVIDENCE_MIN_SCORE
2218:                 && signal.getConfidenceScore() >= WATCH_EVIDENCE_MIN_CONFIDENCE;
2219:     }

2221:     private HardRiskBlock nonAtrHardRiskBlock(TradeSignal signal) {
2222:         if (!signal.isStrategyEntryAllowed()) {
2223:             return HardRiskBlock.block("STRATEGY_ENTRY_BLOCKED", "Selected market strategy does not allow entry.");
2224:         }
2225:         if (!signal.isBtcContextEntryAllowed()) {
2226:             return HardRiskBlock.block("BTC_CONTEXT_BLOCKED", "BTC context issued an entry veto.");
2227:         }
2228:         if (!signal.isDerivativesEntryAllowed()) {
2229:             return HardRiskBlock.block("DERIVATIVES_BLOCKED", "Funding/open-interest positioning issued an entry veto.");
2230:         }
2231:         // With wall-lifecycle intelligence, TARGET_BLOCKED should only remain false-entry-allowed
2232:         // when the wall is genuinely strong/relevant. Weakening walls no longer reach this hard veto.
2233:         if (!signal.isLiquidityEntryAllowed()
2234:                 && signal.getLiquidityStatus() == LiquidityContextStatus.TARGET_BLOCKED) {
2235:             return HardRiskBlock.block("STRONG_TARGET_WALL",
2236:                     "A strong nearby target-side wall is still active; execution remains blocked until fresh order-book evidence changes.");
2237:         }
2238:         return HardRiskBlock.none();
2239:     }

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md lines 1-125 =====
1: # Crypto AI Trader ƒ?" Architecture
2: 
3: ## FIX-156 Replay boundary correction ƒ?" 2026-10-16
4: 
5: Replay has a dedicated `com.crypto.regression.replay` boundary foundation. It
6: holds the frozen policy-profile contract, Replay-only sizing/minimum-order/SELL
7: policy primitives, and an operation-aware gateway for the narrowly permitted
8: `fix122_evaluation` diagnostic operations. This foundation remains incomplete:
9: the existing shadow Replay workflow has not yet been fully rewired away from
10: wallet settings, live Binance constraints, shared BUY validation, or legacy
11: test-table output. Production runtime and trading behavior remain unchanged.
12: 
13: V110 was confirmed already applied and was restored unchanged. Any new
14: Replay-owned schema is forward-only V111; it creates profile-detail, run
15: certificate, and price-coverage records without changing an existing table.
16: 
17: Status labels used in this document:
18: 
19: - **Current** ƒ?" present in the repository today.
20: - **Pending** ƒ?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
21: - **Legacy/shared** ƒ?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
22:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
23: 
24: ## Current release status
25: 
26: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
27: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
28: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
29: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
30: 
31: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
32: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
33: 
34: **FIX-156 is in progress and Replay-only.** A foundation exists for immutable
35: Replay policy profiles, Replay-owned evidence/output tables, and a fail-closed
36: Replay SQL allow-list. The existing Replay service has not yet been rewired
37: away from its live wallet/Binance/shared-service dependencies, so FIX-156 is
38: not complete or deployable.
39: 
40: ## System purpose
41: 
42: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
43: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
44: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
45: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
46: 
47: ## Runtime and data ownership
48: 
49: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
50: |---|---|---|
51: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
52: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
53: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
54: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
55: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
56: 
57: ## Current primary flow
58: 
59: ```text
60: Binance WebSocket kline
61:   -> BinanceKlineService
62:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
63:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
64:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
65:       -> observer transaction
66:       -> close-dispatch transaction
67:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
68:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
69:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
70:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
71:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
72:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
73:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
74: ```
75: 
76: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
77: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
78: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
79: 
80: ## Current core persistence
81: 
82: | Table/domain | Purpose |
83: |---|---|
84: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
85: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
86: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
87: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
88: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
89: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
90: 
91: ## Legacy/shared-market path
92: 
93: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
94: 
95: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
96: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
97: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
98: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
99: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
100: 
101: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
102: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
103: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
104: 
105: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
106: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
107: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
108: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
109: 
110: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
111: 
112: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
113: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
114: path must never run together.
115: 
116: Implemented safeguards:
117: 
118: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
119:   closed candle.
120: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
121:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
122: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
123:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
124: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
125:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md lines 126-250 =====
126: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
127:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
128: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
129: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
130:   query retired shared-market tables.
131: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
132:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
133:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
134:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
135:   candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
136:   the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
137: - Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
138:   boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
139:   not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
140:   analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
141:   repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
142:   gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
143:   provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
144:   and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
145:   case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
146:   not proof that the gap is closed.
147: - Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
148:   duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
149:   REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
150:   terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
151:   a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
152:   failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
153: - The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
154:   not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
155:   queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
156:   protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
157: - Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
158:   V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
159:   pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
160:   request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
161:   unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
162: - Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
163:   protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
164: - Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
165:   database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
166:   separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
167:   HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
168:   General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
169:   clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
170:   Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
171:   transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
172:   REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
173: - An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
174:   quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
175:   the controlled drain window.
176: 
177: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
178: 
179: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
180: 
181: ```text
182: PENDING
183:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
184:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
185:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
186: ```
187: 
188: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
189: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
190: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
191: 
192: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
193: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
194: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
195: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
196: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
197: 
198: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
199: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
200: 
201: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
202: 
203: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
204: 
205: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
206: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
207: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
208: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
209: remains unchanged.
210: 
211: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
212: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
213: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
214: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
215: 
216: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
217: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
218: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
219: 
220: ## Deployment posture
221: 
222: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
223: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
224: 
225: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
226: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
227: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
228: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
229: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
230: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
231: compile the Java 21 project.
232: 
233: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
234: without explicit user approval.
235: 
236: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
237: 
238: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
239: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
240: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
241: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
242: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
243: 
244: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
245: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
246: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
247: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
248: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
249: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
250: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md lines 251-320 =====
251: must stay `false`.
252: 
253: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
254: contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
255: mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
256: starting.
257: 
258: ### Shared Binance REST admission
259: 
260: `BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
261: immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
262: response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
263: the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
264: calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
265: of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.
266: 
267: ### Outcome-schema startup invariant
268: 
269: The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
270: terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
271: though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
272: runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
273: table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
274: when they attempt their first outcome write.
275: # FIX-154 implementation update ƒ?" 2026-10-10
276: 
277: `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
278: the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
279: entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
280: ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
281: for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
282: uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
283: initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
284: the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.
285: 
286: `buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
287: best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
288: execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
289: be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
290: existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
291: that guard has already rejected an add.
292: 
293: `GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
294: blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.
295: 
296: ## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation ƒ?" enabled 2026-10-10
297: 
298: In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
299: five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
300: missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
301: the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
302: An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
303: plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
304: dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
305: ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

===== codex-docs/FIX-154_IMPLEMENTATION_HANDOFF.md lines 1-120 =====
1: # FIX-154 ƒ?" Implementation Handoff for Code Review
2: 
3: Date: 2026-10-10  
4: Status: implemented in the local working tree; not deployed.
5: 
6: ## Scope
7: 
8: FIX-154 changes only higher-timeframe BUY authority. It does not enable or
9: change FIX-153 reconciliation/runtime behavior.
10: 
11: The objective is to distinguish trusted, strong bearish 1h evidence from
12: unknown or weaker evidence without changing a stored historical signal.
13: 
14: ## Implemented policy
15: 
16: 1. The current 1m BUY still requires fresh 5m and 1h context.
17: 2. A bearish 5m returns the existing `5M_BEARISH_VETO` first.
18: 3. A bearish 1h with `data_quality_valid` `NULL` or `false` returns
19:    `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
20: 4. A trusted 1h `STRONG_SELL` with `confidence_score >= 70` returns
21:    `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
22: 5. A trusted 1h `SELL`, or `STRONG_SELL` with confidence below 70, is a
23:    SOFT conflict. It passes effective 1h `NEUTRAL` to profile logic and caps
24:    every allowed initial entry at 25%.
25: 6. A non-bearish 1h with unknown quality keeps its raw decision; FIX-154 does
26:    not tighten bullish/non-bearish authority merely because historical quality
27:    is absent.
28: 7. Existing progressive/add logic retains its raw 1h requirement. A SOFT
29:    conflict cannot authorize an add. Optional `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence
30:    is observational only.
31:

33:

40:   `data_quality_valid`.

42:   candle-close-based age, quality evidence, the hard-confirmation marker,
43:   cap, legacy veto and final result.

===== codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md lines 1-125 =====
1: # FIX-154 Г?" Trusted higher-timeframe BUY validation
2: 
3: Status: implementation design approved. FIX-154 is scheduled before the
4: remaining FIX-153 runtime release work.
5: 
6: ## Purpose and boundary
7: 
8: FIX-154 replaces the current binary fresh-1h bearish veto with a trusted
9: higher-timeframe assessment. It does not relax the existing 5m confirmation
10: requirement, risk/reward result, current-1m BTC protection, or profile rules
11: except where a raw 1h decision would otherwise act as an untrusted veto.
12: 
13: The legacy `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md` is an unrelated shared-market
14: readiness design and is not an implementation source for this fix.
15: 
16: ## Current integration points to replace
17: 
18: The 1h/5m blocks in all three paths below must be replaced as a whole by one
19: shared assessment call. Do not add this decision to `FinalDecisionService`.
20: 
21: 1. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuy()` Г?" direct 1m BUYs.
22: 2. `TradeExecutionValidationService.validateBuyContext()` Г?" accumulated/setup
23:    authority, whose reference may be a 5m signal.
24: 3. `OpportunityConsolidationService.evaluate()` Г?" consolidated BUYs.
25: 
26: Each replacement owns the current lookup, freshness, 5m veto, and 1h veto
27: block in that method. The remaining profile/consolidation logic consumes the
28: assessment's effective context; it must not reread the raw 1h decision as an
29: independent veto.
30: 
31: ## Immutable signal evidence
32: 
33: Migration `V107` adds:
34: 
35: ```sql
36: ALTER TABLE trade_signal ADD COLUMN data_quality_valid BOOLEAN NULL;
37: ```
38: 
39: `AnalysisService` writes `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` through the
40: existing `TradeSignal.builder()` call for every newly generated signal on every
41: interval. Historic rows are never backfilled: `NULL` means unknown/invalid.
42: 
43: FIX-154 does not depend on `signal_mode` or any other FIX-153 runtime/schema
44: feature. `data_quality_valid = true` is sufficient evidence eligibility for
45: this release; false or `NULL` is unusable. Historical-only exclusion is added
46: later with FIX-153, after its own migration/runtime release is approved.
47: 
48: Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` is audit evidence only. It is never a
49: FIX-154 usability or hard-veto predicate. The current 1m BTC guard is unchanged.
50: 
51: ## Time-bounded selection and fixed freshness
52: 
53: For a request with reference signal `R`, use `R.generatedAt` as `evaluatedAt`.
54: Select the latest same-symbol 5m and 1h signal such that both its generated
55: time and its candle close are at or before `evaluatedAt`; no wall-clock lookup
56: is permitted. Replay supplies the same historical reference time.
57: 
58: Existing fixed freshness limits remain authoritative:
59: 
60: | Interval | Maximum age |
61: |---|---:|
62: | 5m | 20 minutes |
63: | 1h | 3 hours |
64: 
65: `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` and `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` retain their existing
66: meaning and priority when their respective interval is missing or stale. A
67: stale signal is not quietly converted into an allowed BUY just because it is
68: `NOT_TRUSTED` for FIX-154.
69: 
70: ## Usability and 1h strength
71: 
72: A fresh row is usable for FIX-154 only if `data_quality_valid = true`.
73: 
74: FIX-154 v1 does not invent ADX/DMI/VWAP/CMF evidence, because those indicators
75: are not currently persisted. Its fixed strength definition is:
76: 
77: ```text
78: HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE = 1h decision == STRONG_SELL
79:                          AND confidence_score >= 70
80: SOFT_CONFLICT          = fresh, quality-valid 1h decision == SELL
81:                          OR STRONG_SELL with confidence_score below 70
82: NOT_TRUSTED            = fresh but quality-invalid/unknown/historical-only 1h
83:                          OR fresh non-bearish 1h
84: ```
85: 
86: `confidence_score` is the persisted effective confidence. `raw_confidence_score`
87: is explicitly not used. The constant `70` is a named FIX-154 configuration
88: default, persisted in the audit row, and may change only through a reviewed
89: strategy change with Replay regression.
90: 
91: ## Exact 1h A- 5m decision matrix
92: 
93: First apply existing missing/stale checks. Only then apply this table.
94: 
95: | 1h state | 5m state | Final HTF result before profile | Profile inputs |
96: |---|---|---|---|
97: | missing/stale | any | existing `MISSING_1H_CONTEXT` rejection | none |
98: | any | missing/stale | existing `MISSING_5M_CONFIRMATION` rejection | none |
99: | NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
100: | NOT_TRUSTED bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | `1H_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
101: | NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
102: | NOT_TRUSTED non-bearish from false/NULL quality | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |
103: | SOFT_CONFLICT | bearish | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
104: | SOFT_CONFLICT | bullish/neutral/watch | continue to profile, cap resulting first entry at 25% | effective 1h=`NEUTRAL`, raw 5m |
105: | HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bearish, quality-valid | legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO`; audit `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` | none |
106: | HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE | bullish/neutral/watch, quality-valid | `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO` | none |
107: | fresh, quality-valid non-bearish 1h | non-bearish | continue to profile | raw 1h, raw 5m |
108: 
109: The profile call produces the final decision for every `continue to profile`
110: row. It may still return its existing insufficient-confirmation code. It must
111: not consult the raw 1h row after this mapping.
112: 
113: `5m quality=false/NULL` does not alter the legacy 5m fallback. A fresh raw
114: bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO`, exactly as today. It merely cannot
115: act as new FIX-154 hard-veto confirmation or a soft-conflict release.
116: 
117: The veto order is fixed: a bearish 5m returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any
118: trusted-1h result. If the 1h is hard, audit records `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT`
119: on that legacy outcome. When 5m is not bearish, a hard trusted 1h returns
120: `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`. The only deliberate lasting relaxation is a trusted
121: SOFT bearish 1h: an otherwise allowed initial entry is capped at 25%.
122: 
123: ## Profile contract
124: 
125: `conservativeBuy`, `balancedBuy`, `balancedBuyWithNeutralFiveException`, and

===== codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md lines 126-300 =====
126: `aggressiveBuy` receive the assessment's effective 1h/5m contextГ?"not raw
127: selected 1h evidence. A SOFT conflict maps to `NEUTRAL`, never `WATCH`, then
128: the validation result is capped with `min(profilePositionPercent, 25)`.
129: This means the neutral-5m/direct-BUY exception still requires a genuinely
130: bullish 1h and cannot be unlocked by SOFT evidence. An untrusted non-bearish
131: 1h retains its raw decision so deployment does not accidentally tighten current
132: bullish authority.
133: 
134: The assessment result carries `maxInitialPercent`: `25` for `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
135: otherwise no cap. Every caller that selects a size must use
136: `min(itsCalculatedPercent, maxInitialPercent)` when the cap is present. This
137: includes direct validation, `validateBuyContext()` callers (the 5m-trigger
138: path and accumulated evidence), and
139: `OpportunityConsolidationService.consolidatedPositionPercent()`.
140: 
141: The raw selected signals remain audit evidence. Existing 5m requirements inside
142: profiles remain unchanged. HARD and untrusted-bearish outcomes never call a
143: profile method.

145: ### Add-on contract
146: 
147: `ExecutionIntelligenceService.progressivePositionDecision()` already reads raw
148: 1h evidence and requires `WATCH` or bullish support for `SCOUT_ENTRY`,
149: `CONFIRMATION_ADD`, and `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`. Keep that check unchanged and
150: outside the three replacement paths; it already blocks HARD, SOFT, and
151: untrusted-bearish 1h states. Do not route it through effective FIX-154 context,
152: which could widen behavior. When that existing check rejects while the assessed
153: band is SOFT, record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` as audit/metric evidence only.
154: 
155: ## Append-only execution audit
156: 
157: FIX-154 is evaluated during execution/consolidation, not while a signal is
158: saved. Migration `V107` creates append-only `buy_htf_assessment_audit`:
159: 
160: - primary key ID;
161: - reference signal ID, evaluation path (`DIRECT_BUY`, `BUY_CONTEXT`,
162:   `CONSOLIDATION`, `PROGRESSIVE_TREND_ADD`, `CONFIRMATION_ADD`,
163:   `ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE_ADD`) and `evaluated_at`; no unique constraint;
164: - raw/effective 1h and 5m signal IDs, decisions, signal modes, candle closes,
165:   ages, data-quality values, and usability reasons;
166: - configured confidence threshold, band, legacy-veto result, profile context,
167:   final result/reason, Production/Replay mode, and creation timestamp.
168: 
169: The audit is written best-effort after the relevant execution/consolidation
170: decision is known. A duplicate or persistence failure is logged and increments
171: `fix154.audit_write_failure_count`; it must never roll back, reject, or alter a
172: trading decision. It is evidence onlyГ?"not a fence or an input to later trading.
173: 
174: ## Deployment behavior
175: 
176: Immediately after deployment all pre-V107 1h/5m rows have
177: `data_quality_valid=NULL`. They are treated as `NOT_TRUSTED`; they cannot make a
178: new permissive decision: a bearish unknown 1h remains blocked, while a
179: non-bearish unknown 1h retains its existing profile authority. The health view
180: must expose `fix154.untrusted_bearish_1h_block_count` and
181: `fix154.audit_write_failure_count`, `fix154.soft_capped_entry_count`, and
182: `fix154.soft_add_blocked_count`, grouped by execution path.
183: 
184: ## Migration ordering and FIX-153 independence
185: 
186: The configured local database has V106 as its latest applied Flyway migration.
187: Therefore FIX-154 reserves `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql`.
188: Before this migration is applied anywhere, rename the currently unreleased
189: `V107__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql` to V108. Never rename or edit
190: an applied migration. Do not enable Flyway `outOfOrder`; deployment order is
191: made explicit instead.
192: 
193: ## Required regression suite
194: 
195: - All three integration paths use the shared service and no longer contain raw
196:   `1H_BEARISH_VETO` branches.
197: - `STRONG_SELL` + `confidence_score >= 70` and bearish fresh valid 5m produces
198:   legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` with `HARD_CONFIRMATION_PRESENT` audit; the same 1h
199:   with non-bearish 5m produces `HTF_TRUSTED_BEARISH_VETO`.
200: - Strong/soft/untrusted 1h plus bullish, neutral, watch, bearish, missing,

202:   final profile result.

204:   priority and codes.
205: - BNB #150a: persisted historical 1h bearish row with `NULL` quality =>
206:   `NOT_TRUSTED` and `1H_BEARISH_VETO`.
207: - BNB #150b: Replay regenerates that same 1h with quality evidence and it is
208:   SOFT; no trusted hard veto is emitted and the final expected result is
209:   `REJECTED_RISK_REWARD` when the risk/reward guard fails.
210: - Historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` does not change usability; current
211:   1m BTC behavior is unchanged.
212: - FIX-154 migration succeeds with no `signal_mode` column and its assessment
213:   has no FIX-153 schema/runtime dependency.
214: - No future signal/candle is selected in Production or Replay; both produce
215:   equivalent audit evidence for the same historical inputs.
216: - Pre-migration `NULL` quality is never treated as valid.
217: - Repeated execution/consolidation evaluations append audit rows and audit
218:   failure leaves the business decision intact.
219: - A SOFT initial entry never exceeds 25% through progressive, confirmation, or
220:   accumulated-evidence or consolidation sizing; existing progressive raw-1h
221:   support rules block add stages while the 1h remains bearish.
222: 
223: ## Release conditions
224: 
225: Run Java 21 unit/integration tests, migration tests, Production/Replay parity,

229: ## Revision addendum Г?" Round 5 approval conditions
230: 
231: This addendum governs where it differs from earlier wording.
232: 
233: ### Release build decision
234: 
235: The FIX-154 release build includes `V108__fix_153_local_candle_reconciliation.sql`
236: after the current unreleased FIX-153 V107 migration is renumbered to V108.
237: This is required even with reconciliation disabled: `candle_analysis_outcome`
238: is used by ordinary live signal processing, terminal worker outcomes, and the
239: closed-candle detector. Omitting V108 would cause missing-table SQL failures;
240: in particular, a normal signal-processing completion would roll back because
241: its outcome write is inside the processing transaction.
242: 
243: Including V108 ships FIX-153 reconciliation *schema only*. The reconciliation
244: runtime remains disabled and retains all of its separate release gates.
245: 
246: ### No FIX-153 provenance dependency
247: 
248: Ignore earlier references to historical-only status or signal modes in the
249: FIX-154 band definition and audit payload. FIX-154 v1 eligibility depends only
250: on time-bounded selection and `data_quality_valid = true`; `signal_mode` is not
251: read, required, or stored by the FIX-154 audit.
252: 
253: ### Effective context is transient
254: 
255: The assessment returns immutable effective decision values separately from the
256: loaded `TradeSignal` entities. No code may call `setDecision`, mutate, merge,
257: or save a selected 1h/5m signal to obtain effective `NEUTRAL` context. Profile
258: methods receive explicit effective decisions (or an immutable assessment
259: context), while the raw entity remains audit evidence only. A regression test
260: must load a SOFT 1h signal, execute the assessment/profile path, flush/clear,
261: and prove its stored decision is unchanged.
262: 
263: ### Progressive-path evidence is observational
264: 
265: `progressivePositionDecision()` retains its existing raw-1h support check as
266: the only decision authority for scout/add paths. If that existing check passes,
267: FIX-154 performs no assessment lookup and writes no progressive audit row. If
268: it rejects because raw 1h is bearish, a best-effort audit-only assessment may
269: record `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` when the band is SOFT. That observation must not
270: alter the rejection, allow an add, cause a database lookup on passing paths, or
271: propagate an audit failure into the trading decision.
272: 
273: V108 ships with FIX-154 because the current runtime writes
274: `candle_analysis_outcome`. Reconciliation remains disabled.
275: 
276: ### Round 5 text clarification
277: 
278: This addendum removes any earlier contrary implication: FIX-154 v1 has no
279: `signal_mode` or historical-only predicate or audit field. Effective 1h/5m
280: decisions are separate immutable values and never mutate a loaded signal.
281: Progressive-path assessment is audit-only after its existing raw-1h check has
282: already rejected; it is skipped entirely when that check passes and never feeds
283: the scout/add decision.
284:

286: 
287: The approved FIX-154 design is now implemented in the working tree.
288: 
289: - `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql` creates the nullable signal
290:   quality evidence and append-only assessment audit table. No historical value
291:   is fabricated or backfilled.
292: - `AnalysisService` persists `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new
293:   signal.
294: - `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` owns the hard/soft/untrusted decision
295:   matrix. It returns immutable effective decisions and does not mutate a JPA
296:   signal.
297: - Direct BUY validation, accumulated-evidence authority, and opportunity
298:   consolidation invoke that one service. A SOFT result uses effective 1h
299:   `NEUTRAL` and applies the 25% initial-position cap. The legacy bearish-5m
300:   veto retains precedence. Progressive adds retain their existing raw 1h

12: - Scope: read-only check of all 21 enabled symbols in `crypto_ai.candle`, over the two-hour window ending at the database UTC clock.
13: - Current state: all 63 enabled symbol/interval lanes (1m, 5m, 1h) have closed-candle records and current live ingestion. The newest 1m candle was 54 seconds old at the check.

This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.
15: - Impact: current live signals are being generated, but the missing historical 1m candles should be reconciled before relying on uninterrupted historical indicator lineage for that startup window. No database data or code was changed by this check.

174: # Replay-versus-Production parity audit ƒ?" 2026-10-10
175: 
176: - Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
177:   the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
178:   database data, or Replay result was changed.
179: - Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
180:   `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
181:   through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
182:   `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
183:   profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
184:   classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
185:   consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
186:   visible, matching the documented live ordering.
187: - Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
188:   identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
189:   manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
190:   position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
191:   reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
192:   `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
193:   real execution latency.
194: - Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
195:   persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
196:   context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
197:   stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
198:   whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
199:   are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
200:   required historical evidence exists and is reported.
201: - Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
202:   `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
203:   does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
204:   protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
205:   are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
206:   should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
207: - Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
208:   parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
209:   Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
210:   Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
211:   price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
212: - Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
213:   and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
214:   evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
215:   to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
216:   scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
217:   release-equivalence certificate.

===== codex-docs/TRADE_INVESTIGATION_AGENT.md lines 1-160 =====
1: # Trade Investigation Agent
2: 
3: ## Role
4: 
5: Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
6: happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
7: rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
8: at that time.
9: 
10: Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
11: database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.
12: 
13: ## Required input
14: 
15: Obtain or resolve:
16: 
17: - Environment and application: local, test, or production.
18: - Database host, port, and schema.
19: - One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
20:   a bounded approximate entry-time window.
21: - Optional question or suspected issue.
22: 
23: Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
24: level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
25: columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
26: `crypto_ai_v2`.
27: 
28: ## Evidence rules
29: 
30: - Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
31: - Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
32: - Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
33: - Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
34: - Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
35:   conflicting evidence**.
36: - Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
37: - A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
38:   transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
39:   committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
40:   no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
41: - Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
42:   no configuration history exists, report that limitation.
43: 
44: ## Investigation workflow
45: 
46: ### 1. Resolve identity and environment
47: 
48: Link the trade reference to, where available:
49: 
50: - `paper_position`
51: - `wallet_trade`
52: - entry and exit `trade_signal`
53: - `signal_processing_work`
54: - wallet/position execution records
55: 
56: Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
57: local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.
58: 
59: ### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle
60: 
61: Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
62: requires it. Collect:
63: 
64: | Evidence | Establishes |
65: | --- | --- |
66: | `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
67: | `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
68: | `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
69: | `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
70: | `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
71: | `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
72: | configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
73: | application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |
74: 
75: Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.
76: 
77: ### 3. Reconstruct the timeline
78: 
79: Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:
80: 
81: `candle closed ƒ+' signal saved ƒ+' work claimed ƒ+' locks acquired ƒ+' entry committed ƒ+' protection observations ƒ+' exit decision ƒ+' exit committed`
82: 
83: Measure separately:
84: 
85: - candle close to signal generation;
86: - signal generation to processing/entry;
87: - observation time to protection completion;
88: - exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.
89: 
90: Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
91: or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.
92: 
93: ### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality
94: 
95: Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:
96: 
97: - signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
98: - final decision and entry-allowed result;
99: - BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
100:   strategy gates that applied at that time;
101: - correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
102: - quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.
103: 
104: Return two separate conclusions:
105: 
106: - **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
107: - **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
108:   evidence.
109: 
110: Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
111: as information the entry decision had.
112: 
113: ### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior
114: 
115: For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
116: `PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
117: gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
118: evidence.
119: 
120: For each relevant accepted observation:
121: 
122: 1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
123: 2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
124: 3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
125:    wallet execution record.
126: 4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
127:    lock waits, or pool pressure.
128: 
129: Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
130: take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.
131: 
132: ### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality
133: 
134: Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
135: Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
136: the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
137: the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.
138: 
139: ### 7. Reconcile financial results
140: 
141: Recalculate using execution records:
142: 
143: - bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
144: - gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
145: - partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.
146: 
147: Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.
148: 
149: ### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully
150: 
151: Classify findings as one or more of:
152: 
153: - strategy decision;
154: - data freshness or coverage;
155: - analysis delay;
156: - execution failure;
157: - protection failure;
158: - database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
159: - accounting/UI discrepancy;
160: - insufficient evidence.

===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md lines 1-60 =====
1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
2: 
3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
8:   capped at 25%.
9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
11:   never authorizes an add.
12: 
13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
14: 
15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
16:   the data-quality gate.
17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
21:   generation after a repaired quality window.

- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** The source computes Entry Quality from only evidence at or before the signal: 30-minute price expansion, ATR extension, opportunity age, current reward/risk, recent rejection-zone proximity, volatility regime, and stop-side liquidity exposure. It is recalculated against the fresh execution-time price, and a BUY is cancelled if that price is at/below stop or at/above target. ### DB_REQUEST

QueryId: DBQ-CODEX-I5-001  
Purpose: Establish the actual schema and bounded entry evidence for exactly the six frozen positions, including entry-signal, processing, wallet, and managed-position records. Returned timestamps are UTC; report KSA only as UTC + 03:00.  
SQL:
```sql
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.paper_position;
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.trade_signal;
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.signal_processing_work;
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.wallet_trade;
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.wallet_managed_position;
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.candle;
SHOW CREATE TABLE crypto_ai.technical_indicator;

SELECT p.*
FROM crypto_ai.paper_position p
WHERE p.id IN (182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137)
ORDER BY FIELD(p.id, 182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137);

SELECT
    p.id AS paper_position_id,
    s.*
FROM crypto_ai.paper_position p
JOIN crypto_ai.trade_signal s ON s.id = p.signal_id
WHERE p.id IN (182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137)
ORDER BY FIELD(p.id, 182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137), s.id;

SELECT
    p.id AS paper_position_id,
    w.*
FROM crypto_ai.paper_position p
JOIN crypto_ai.signal_processing_work w ON w.signal_id = p.signal_id
WHERE p.id IN (182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137)
ORDER BY p.id, w.id;

SELECT
    p.id AS paper_position_id,
    wt.*
FROM crypto_ai.paper_position p
JOIN crypto_ai.wallet_trade wt ON wt.signal_id = p.signal_id
WHERE p.id IN (182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137)
ORDER BY p.id, wt.executed_at, wt.id;

SELECT
    p.id AS paper_position_id,
    mp.*
FROM crypto_ai.paper_position p
JOIN crypto_ai.wallet_managed_position mp ON mp.entry_signal_id = p.signal_id
WHERE p.id IN (182, 172, 162, 158, 143, 137)
ORDER BY p.id, mp.opened_at, mp.id;
```
